- `7` **AMD Turns to Samsung to Secure Helios HBM4 Memory Against NVIDIA | Cloud News**
  Lisa Su said the AMD–Samsung HBM4 supply agreement — a March 18 MoU prioritising MI455X — is "nearly" a definitive high-volume contract, with each Helios rack carrying 72 MI455X and ~31TB of HBM4 (~432GB per GPU, which AMD says is ~50% above some Rubin-generation configurations); MI455X is ~320bn transistors blending 2nm and 3nm, ~40 PFLOPS FP4, and Helios is in high-volume production with first commercial shipments targeted before end-Q3 2026 (Azure confirmed as a deployment). This is the sharpest samsung-hbm datapoint yet: a second qualified HBM4 buyer of scale validates Samsung's re-entry and, by implication, caps 000660's HBM4 pricing power at the margin in 2027 negotiations — note the irony against item 2, where Samsung and SK Hynix sold off together on a China narrative while Samsung was winning share. Also read-through to tsmc-nodes: a 2nm/3nm hybrid accelerator entering HVM this quarter is incremental N2 demand ahead of the Rubin curve. Samsung declined to confirm signing, so the contract is unconfirmed; watch for the definitive announcement, disclosed volumes/pricing, and whether Helios actually ships in Q3 — a slip past Q3 would refute the parity timeline.
  Sources: [cloudnews.tech](https://cloudnews.tech/amd-turns-to-samsung-to-secure-helios-hbm4-memory-against-nvidia/)
- `7` **La producción de 2 nm de TSMC avanza a pleno rendimiento; una planta ya ha alcanzado una capacidad de 20.000 obleas mensuales, mientras el fuerte aumento de la demanda podría superar pronto los nivele**
  Per Economic Daily News (via a secondary aggregator), TSMC's Fab 20 has reached 20,000 wpm of N2 and five Taiwan fabs are now dedicated to 2nm, against 175,000 wpm still running at N3 — so N2 is ~11% of N3 volume while already contributing 3% of quarterly revenue with no shipped customer silicon yet, and management has said N2 tape-outs are 4x N3's at the same stage. First N2 products are consumer, not AI: Google Tensor G6 in Pixel 11 (August) and Apple A20 Pro in iPhone 18 (September), with AMD's MI455X and Nvidia's Rubin arriving later. For tsmc-nodes this supports the view that N2 mix ramps faster than N3 did, which is margin-accretive given N2 pricing, and it is a direct read-through to AMD's amd-parity case that MI455X gets a node edge over Blackwell-generation parts; refutation would be any sign the 20k wpm figure is installed rather than output capacity, or slipping Apple/Google launch dates. Treat the single-fab wafer number as unconfirmed — it is second-hand and the article's quarter labelling is internally inconsistent.
  Sources: [masterbitzreviewhn.com](https://masterbitzreviewhn.com/post/la-producci%C3%B3n-de-2-nm-de-tsmc-avanza-a-pleno-rendimiento-una-planta-ya-ha-alcanzado-una-capacidad-d)
- `7` **Samsung to use 2nm process for HBM5 base die as heat challenge grows**
  DigiTimes reports Samsung will build the HBM5 base die on a 2nm gate-all-around foundry process, driven by the power and thermal load that faster HBM puts on the logic die. This is the same disclosure carried in the Korean interview (see next item) and matters for samsung-hbm because the base die is where the foundry/memory integration advantage is contested — SK Hynix outsources its advanced base die to TSMC, so Samsung doing it in-house on 2nm GAA is the concrete form of its turnkey pitch. The same DigiTimes headline list also flags Huawei planning glass-substrate mass production in 2027 for AI chips, which is a direct datapoint for the glass-substrate question and worth chasing separately.
  Sources: [digitimes.com](https://digitimes.com/news/a20260727VL216/samsung-2nm-hbm-performance-hbm4.html)
- `7` **Chinese-Made Immersion DUV Reportedly Starts Production at 5 Units a Year: Customer Qualification Is the Gatekeeper for Replacing ASML | XenoSpectrum**
  The Information reports a state-linked Shanghai firm (team assembled partly from Shanghai Yuliangsheng) has moved domestic immersion DUV from prototypes to production at ~5 units in 2026, targeted for delivery to SMIC, Hua Hong and CXMT, scaling to ~20 units in 2027; the maker, specs, throughput and uptime are undisclosed. The only public Chinese ArF spec — MIIT's 2024 catalog listing at 65nm resolution and 8nm overlay — is far off ASML's NXT:2000i at 38–40nm resolution and 2.5nm overlay, and there is no evidence the two are the same tool; the article's own point is that customer fab qualification, which can take many months, is the gate, not equipment assembly. For wfe-china and export-controls this is an early-stage inflection marker, not a substitution event: five tools cannot move SMIC/CXMT capacity in 2026, but three simultaneous customers create the defect-feedback loop that compresses the qualification curve. The datapoint to watch is whether the 2027 ~20-unit plan is met and whether CXMT qualifies domestic immersion for DRAM production layers — that would be the first genuine threat to the ASML/AMAT/LRCX/KLA China revenue base and, second-order, to CXMT's cost curve in the DRAM cycle.
  Sources: [xenospectrum.com](https://xenospectrum.com/en/china-immersion-duv-production)
- `7` **Meta, BlackRock team up to develop $14B El Paso data centre**
  Meta and BlackRock announced a $14B El Paso data-center JV (previously described by Meta as a >$10B project): BlackRock-managed funds take 80% with ~$4.9B cash and $12.5B of debt, Meta keeps 20%, contributes ~$2.3B of land/construction-in-progress and receives a $1B distribution; the site is designed for 1 GW and starts operations in 2028, one of 28 US sites, against Meta's stated $600B data-center plan through 2028. The mechanism that matters for meta-capex: this is off-balance-sheet/JV financing, so compute capacity can keep compounding even if reported Meta capex decelerates — i.e. do not read a softer capex line on July 29 as softer accelerator/power demand for NVDA, AVGO, MRVL or VRT. Note the source is written as promotional buy-side copy (BLK/META recommendations), so treat the risk framing as opinion, not reported fact. Confirm via the 8-K/press release terms and whether Meta guides 2027 capex ex-JV; refute if management says JV capacity substitutes for, rather than adds to, owned builds.
  Sources: [invezz.com](https://invezz.com/ng/news/2026/07/28/meta-blackrock-team-up-to-develop-dollar14b-el-paso-data-centre)
- `7` **Cloudflare lets sites block AI bots by purpose — search, agent, or training**
  Cloudflare split AI bot controls into three purpose classes — Search, Agent, Training — available now to all customers including Free, and from September 15, 2026 every newly onboarded domain will block Training and Agent by default on ad-bearing pages, with multi-purpose crawlers (Googlebot, Applebot, BingBot) governed by the strictest matching rule, so they get blocked wherever Training is blocked. This is the concrete escalation of net-act4: defaults, not opt-ins, are what create a bargaining position for Pay-Per-Crawl, and forcing Google's dual-purpose crawler into the Training bucket puts search-index access on the table as leverage — implying real pricing power on crawl if publishers accept the defaults rather than opting out before Sept 15. Tension worth flagging for the agentic-commerce thread: blocking the Agent class (ChatGPT-User, Gemini/Claude-driven browsers) by default cuts the other way on x402/agentic-checkout adoption unless paid, authenticated agent access becomes the sanctioned path — which would be the bullish read for NET as toll collector. Confirm via Cloudflare disclosing Pay-Per-Crawl volumes/opt-out rates after Sept 15 and any Google or OpenAI public pushback; refute if large publishers mass-disable the defaults.
  Sources: [metatalks.ai](https://metatalks.ai/cloudflare-lets-sites-block-ai-bots-by-purpose-search-agent-or-training)
- `7` **Chip stock rout sends investors rushing back to bruised Alphabet stock**
  Alphabet posted Q2 capex of $44.7B and raised FY guidance to $195–205B from $180–190B, with executives flagging a 'significant' further increase in 2027 — a ~$15B midpoint raise, i.e. the opposite of a digestion signal. Against the hyperscaler-guides question and the Jul 28–31 window, this is the cleanest datapoint that the AI capex flywheel is still accelerating into 2027, which underwrites TSM/CoWoS loading, NVDA, AVGO and MRVL custom-silicon volumes (Alphabet TPU program) and VRT power/thermal orders. The tell in the framing is that money rotated into Alphabet while semis sold off, i.e. equities are separating demand-side beneficiaries from supply-chain names on China/lithography fears rather than doubting spend. Refutation would be Microsoft/Amazon guiding capex flat-to-down this week; confirmation would be explicit 2027 dollar ranges from either. *(headline only)*
  Sources: [finance.yahoo.com](https://finance.yahoo.com/markets/stocks/article/chip-stock-rout-sends-investors-rushing-back-to-bruised-alphabet-stock-140503906.html)
- `6` **Nvidia Vera Rubin Shifts AI Infrastructure Beyond GPU Speed, ETDatacenters**
  Nvidia confirmed on July 21 that Vera Rubin NVL72 production is ramping with systems already live at CoreWeave, Google Cloud, Azure and OCI, supported by 350+ factory sites across 30 countries; CoreWeave's DeepSeek-R1 benchmark shows ~10x tokens/sec/MW versus Grace Blackwell NVL72 and Nvidia claims up to 90% lower inference cost per token on specific workloads. The framing shift — tokens per megawatt as the buying metric because grid, power density and cooling are the binding constraints — is the decision-useful part: it moves content and pricing power toward HBM4 (000660, samsung-hbm), advanced packaging/CoWoS (TSM, AMAT, BESI, ONTO), optics and high-speed connectivity (LITE, AVGO, MRVL), and power/thermal (VRT, VICR). Treat the 10x and 90% figures as vendor/workload-specific, not fleet-average; a 10x tokens/MW gain also cuts both ways — it is a demand pull for Rubin content but, if real at scale, reduces racks needed per unit of token output. Confirm/refute for rubin-ramp: NVDA's next datacenter mix disclosure showing Rubin revenue crossover, CoWoS-L allocation commentary, and independent (MLPerf or third-party) reproduction of the tokens/MW claim.
  Sources: [datacenters.economictimes.indiatimes.com](https://datacenters.economictimes.indiatimes.com/news/ai-compute-infrastructure/nvidia-vera-rubin-shifts-ai-infrastructure-beyond-gpu-speed/132674544)
- `6` **AMD Unveils New AI GPUs and EPYC CPUs for Data Centres | Data Centre Magazine**
  At Advancing AI, AMD launched 6th-gen EPYC 9006 (SP7/SP8/9006X/LP), the Instinct MI400 family (MI455X for hyperscale, MI430X for sovereign/HPC) and ROCm.ai, claiming up to 3.3x faster inference and 2.4x faster training versus ROCm 7 on the same hardware — a software-only TCO gain, which is the most important number here because ROCm maturity, not FLOPS, has been the gating factor on AMD's hyperscaler share. Lisa Su framed demand with two datapoints worth carrying: the industry now processes >35 quadrillion AI tokens/month and ~60% of AI capacity is inference, which supports the CPU-orchestration attach story for EPYC alongside GPUs (agentic workloads = dozens of steps per request). For amd-parity, the transmission is share shift versus NVDA at the inference margin plus a pull on 2nm/3nm wafers (TSM) and HBM4; the confirmatory evidence is MLPerf submissions on MI455X and named multi-gigawatt customer commitments, while continued absence of verified MLPerf results would mark the 3.3x as internal-benchmark noise.
  Sources: [datacentremagazine.com](https://datacentremagazine.com/news/amd-unveils-new-ai-gpus-and-epyc-cpus-for-data-centres)
- `6` **AMD Helios integra 72 GPU MI455X en un rack para IA**
  AMD's own Performance Labs comparison (June–July 2026, published specs) puts Helios at +15% peak FP4, +50% HBM capacity, +6% HBM bandwidth and +50% scale-out bandwidth versus an NVIDIA Vera Rubin NVL72 rack, with up to 30% more tokens per dollar using a Kimi K2 Thinking workload and projected GPU hourly pricing; the rack is 18 compute trays x4 GPUs = 72 accelerators, 2.9 EF FP4, 1.4 EF FP8, 31TB HBM4 and 1.7 PB/s memory bandwidth. The single most important line for mrvl-fabric is that Helios uses UALink-over-Ethernet for scale-up and standards-based Ethernet (Ultra Ethernet Consortium) for scale-out with Pensando Vulcano 800 NICs — the first shipping rack-scale endorsement of the open scale-up stack against NVLink, which is the demand precondition for MRVL's scale-up SerDes/switch and retimer content and for the ESUN/UEC ecosystem broadly. Two cautions: the aggregate memory-bandwidth figures are internally inconsistent across sources (1.7 PB/s here vs 19.6 TB/s/GPU x72 ≈ 1.4 PB/s implied in the AMD-Samsung item), and all comparisons are theoretical spec math plus modelled pricing, not measured throughput. Confirm/refute: MLPerf or hyperscaler-published tokens/$ on shipped Helios racks, and whether AMD names third-party scale-up silicon suppliers (MRVL/AVGO/Astera) in the Vulcano/UALink path — a fully in-house Pensando/AMD fabric would blunt the MRVL read.
  Sources: [www.tabulado.net](https://www.tabulado.net/amd-helios-integra-72-gpu-mi455x-en-un-rack-para-ia/)
- `6` **Corning Q2 2026 slides: upgraded Springboard Plan targets $40B by 2030 By Investing.com**
  Corning's Q2 2026 deck upgrades its Springboard Plan to a $40B-by-2030 revenue target and, more relevantly, introduces a Photonics Market-Access Platform aimed at a $10B opportunity by 2030 explicitly for co-packaged and near-packaged optics — optics moving 'inside the box'. A materials supplier sizing a discrete $10B CPO/NPO opportunity out to 2030 is a supply-side validation of the cpo adoption-timing question, implying volume ramps are being planned for rather than piloted. Read-through is mixed for coverage: it supports CPO-attached content growth at AVGO, NVDA switch platforms and LITE/AAOI component demand, but 'inside the box' optics is precisely the architecture that compresses pluggable transceiver dollar content over time. Confirmation would be Corning naming switch-OEM design wins or capacity commitments with dates; a $10B 2030 TAM with no attached customer or capex figure is still a slide, not an order book. *(headline only)*
  Sources: [za.investing.com](https://za.investing.com/news/company-news/corning-q2-2026-slides-upgraded-springboard-plan-targets-40b-by-2030-93CH-4390350)
- `5` **Nvidia produce sus primeros chips GB300 en Estados Unidos, aunque sigue dependiendo de Taiwán para su empaquetado CoWoS**
  TSMC has begun producing Nvidia GB300 dies on N4 at Fab 21 in Phoenix, but the wafers are still air-freighted to Taiwan for CoWoS attach of the HBM3E stacks — i.e. US onshoring covers front-end only, and advanced packaging remains the single-point Taiwan dependency. TSMC plans US CoWoS/SoIC lines and Amkor is building packaging/test in Arizona, with the article projecting a fully domestic flow only once 2nm/1.6nm and local packaging land around 2028. For the cowos and tsmc-nodes questions this quantifies the bottleneck's location rather than its size: packaging capacity, not Arizona wafer starts, gates US-made AI systems, and it implies continued Taiwan concentration risk in any tariff or cross-strait scenario despite the political headline. Watch for a dated TSMC Arizona CoWoS/SoIC construction start or Amkor qualification milestone as the confirming datapoint; absent that, treat 'US-made GB300' as marketing.
  Sources: [profesionalreview.com](https://profesionalreview.com/2026/07/27/nvidia-chips-gb300-estados-unidos)
- `5` **Meta is backing 10 gas plants to power data centers – and just quit a global renewables pledge | Electrek**
  Meta has been dropped from/left RE100 because its power plan no longer meets the criteria: the Hyperion campus in Richland Parish, Louisiana went from three Entergy gas plants (~2.26 GW) to ten totaling ~7.5 GW after a March 2026 agreement added seven combined-cycle plants (>5.2 GW), plus a 200 MW behind-the-meter gas project in Ohio; the NYT detail that no insurer would fully cover Hyperion (size, flood plain) is a new project-risk datapoint. For coverage this is a physical-capacity confirmation behind meta-capex: dedicated firm generation of this scale is only rational if Meta intends multi-GW compute ramps through the late 2020s, which supports VRT (power/thermal), NVDA/AVGO/MRVL accelerator demand and the Hyperion 5 GW figure cited elsewhere. The mechanism to watch is that gas-turbine and interconnect lead times, not chips, become the binding constraint — implying capex conversion to deployed compute slips later than headline dollars suggest. Confirmation would be Entergy filings/CCGT COD dates and any Meta commentary on power-limited ramp on the July 29 call; refutation would be Meta trimming the Louisiana plant slate.
  Sources: [electrek.co](https://electrek.co/2026/07/27/meta-is-backing-10-gas-plants-and-just-quit-a-global-renewables-pledge)

- `3` **Meta Q2 2026 Earnings: AI Tools Drive Ad Revenue to $55 Billion**
  Meta reports Q2 2026 after the close on July 29 with consensus at $60.18B against a wide $58–61B company guide, and 2026 capex guided to $125–145B directed mainly at AI chips, data centers and power; note the source's own headline ($55B ad revenue) conflicts with the $60.18B consensus in the body, so treat the revenue figure as unreliable. New operating detail: AI recommendation improvements are credited with ~6% higher conversion rates, and Threads at 141.5M daily mobile actives has passed X at 125M — both feed the monetization-justifies-capex argument (meta-ai). This is the first print in the investor's Jul 28–31 hyperscaler-guide window, to be read against the TSM sell-the-beat question: the decision variable is the 2026 capex range narrowing and any 2027 framing, since the $125–145B band spans ~$20B of accelerator/networking/power orders for NVDA, AVGO, MRVL and VRT. The source's suggestion that a capex *reduction* signals confidence is backwards for the supply chain — a cut would be the bearish datapoint for coverage names; watch instead for a raise to the upper half plus an explicit 2027 'significant increase' comment as Alphabet just gave.
  Sources: [inews.zoombangla.com](https://inews.zoombangla.com/meta-q2-2026-earnings)
- `2` **NVIDIA, Cerebras, and the Memory Wall: How the AI Bottleneck Shifted from Compute to Bandwidth - FourWeekMBA**
  This is analysis, not new reporting: it restates that HBM is effectively sold out through 2026 at SK Hynix, Samsung and Micron, that CoWoS and leading-edge nodes are the consecutive chokepoints behind it, and frames decode (sequential, bandwidth-bound, ~140GB of weight movement per token for a 70B model) rather than FLOPS as the binding constraint, with output tokens priced ~5x input as the observable signal. The useful hook is the claim that SK Hynix's late-July earnings are the tightest forward read on HBM capacity — that is the near-term catalyst for 000660 and, by extension, for HBM4 pricing power and the hbm4/samsung-hbm questions. If decode economics really dominate, value migrates to memory bandwidth, packaging and power (000660, 285A, CoWoS suppliers, VRT) and away from pure compute, which is also the structural bull case for high-bandwidth flash (SNDK) and for prefill/decode disaggregation onto Trainium-class XPUs (MRVL, AVGO). No new numbers here, so treat as framing only; confirmation would be SK Hynix guiding 2027 HBM already contracted and CoWoS bookings extending through 2027.
  Sources: [fourweekmba.com](https://fourweekmba.com/ai-nvidia-cerebras-memory-wall-ai-bottleneck/)
- `2` **Speed Kills and What That Means for Microsoft and Meta on July 29 - 24/7 Wall St.**
  Preview of the July 29 Meta/Microsoft prints: Meta has already raised FY26 capex to $125–145B (+46.8% YoY) against Q1 revenue of $56.31B (+33.1%), impressions +19% and price per ad +12%; Microsoft's last quarter was $82.89B (+18.3%) with Azure +40%, a $37B AI run-rate (+123%) and $627B commercial RPO (+99%), on capex +84.4% YoY. Both stocks are down roughly 10% since April 29, and the piece flags Azure clearing the 40–42% consensus (put at 53.5% likelihood) as the swing factor after April's ~10% single-day drop. This is the core of the hyperscaler-guides window: for the AI supply chain (NVDA, AVGO, MRVL, VRT, NBIS, CRWV) the binding variable is not the beat but whether either raises FY27 capex again, and Microsoft explicitly attributing Azure deceleration to data-center construction and GPU delivery timing would be a supply-constrained read — bullish for equipment/power, neutral-to-bearish for MSFT multiple. For meta-capex specifically, another raise above $145B would extend the compute order book that MRVL and AVGO custom-silicon share arguments depend on; a capex hold with softer ad pricing (crowd sees only 38.5% odds of 9–12% ad price growth) would be the first crack. Note the Polymarket beat odds (94.7% Meta, 91% MSFT) are near-certainties and therefore carry no information — the guide, not the quarter, is the trade.
  Sources: [247wallst.com](https://247wallst.com/investing/2026/07/27/speed-kills-and-what-that-means-for-microsoft-and-meta-on-july-29)

- `9` **Teradyne Q2 Earnings: $1.329B Revenue, $2.38 EPS | TER Stock News** → [[Theses/TER - Teradyne]]

The sting is the Q3 guide: $1,200–1,300M revenue and $1.85–2.15 non-GAAP EPS, i.e. sequentially down at the midpoint ($1,250M vs $1,329M) and EPS down ~19% q/q even while CEO Greg Smith calls Q3 demand 'robust AI-related' — a classic sell-the-beat setup unless the decline is purely mix/timing, which the release does not explain. Two coverage-relevant reads: the NAND final-test inflection is an independent corroboration of the nand-cycle thesis (bullish SNDK, and consistent with 000660/285A memory pricing power), and Smith's 'rapid increase in wafer fab equipment investment sets the stage for continued growth in 2027' is a forward WFE signal for AMAT/LRCX/KLA/ONTO/CAMT/TER itself. Confirmation would be Q3 memory-test bookings commentary and Advantest's own HBM/wafer-test guide (hbm-test); refutation would be Q3 shortfall driven by memory rather than Product Test/Robotics, which would reframe the record as a pull-forward.

- `8` **ASML Shares Sink To June Lows As China Begins Mass-Producing Rival Lithography Tools** → [[Theses/AMAT - Applied Materials]]
  The Information reports a Shanghai state-backed effort (teams pulled from Shanghai Yuliangsheng, linked to Huawei's SiCarrier vehicle) has entered mass production of immersion DUV, targeting ~5 systems in 2026 and ~20 in 2027 for SMIC, Hua Hong and CXMT, with the tool in evaluation at SMIC since September 2025, aimed initially at 28nm but engineered to reach 7nm/5nm via multipatterning and sourced mostly domestically; ASML fell 5–8% (~$44B, more than 4x its implied 2026 China revenue) with AMAT, LRCX and KLA down 5–7%, while ASMI fell >7% and BESI ~10%. The units are small versus ASML's ~130 immersion-DUV 2026 capacity (5 units ≈ 3.8%; 20 units ≈ 11.8% of 169 in 2027) and China already fell to 14% of net system revenue from 19% in Q1, so this is a terminal-value/TAM repricing, not a 2026 earnings event. Second-order effects that matter more for coverage: it weakens the leverage of the pending MATCH Act and existing BIS controls (export-controls), supports domestic tool substitution at Naura/AMEC/Skyverse (wfe-china), and by extension pressures the China revenue lines and long-run China share at AMAT, LRCX, KLA, ONTO, CAMT, SIVE, ASMI and BESI even though metrology/inspection and EUV remain unsubstituted. This is closer to an inflection datapoint than incremental news; confirm via SMIC yield/28nm capacity disclosures and whether ASML's next China guide degrades faster than inventory-digestion explains, refute if the 2026 five-unit target slips or the tools stay in evaluation rather than production.
  Sources: [www.hngn.com](https://www.hngn.com/articles/272405/20260728/asml-shares-sink-june-lows-china-begins-mass-producing-rival-lithography-tools.htm) · [ibtimes.co.uk](https://ibtimes.co.uk/chinas-duv-chipmaking-breakthrough-challenges-western-dominance-1810914)

- `8` **China Chipmaking : Mid-2030s at the earliest': Analysts wrote off China's chipmaking ambitions, now the first homegrown machines ship this year | - The Times of India** → [[Theses/SOI - Soitec]]
  The same China DUV story with sharper detail and one source disagreement: this account does not name Aishengna, instead crediting a multi-firm development effort including state-backed Shanghai Yuliangsheng, whose immersion tool SMIC has been testing since September 2025, with some critical parts still Japanese and supplier delays capping 2026 output. The timeline shock is the point — the AI Futures Project in June forecast commercial-scale Chinese immersion DUV in the mid-2030s with ASML at 98.7% share, so the consensus error is roughly a decade, and immersion DUV is the legal ceiling for Chinese fabs (28nm single-exposure, pushed to 7nm via multipatterning at a yield cost — the route SMIC used for its third-gen 7nm). ASML CFO Dassen's July comments (+30% immersion capacity in 2027, another 30% under study for 2028) frame the 5-then-20 unit domestic ramp as immaterial to near-term volumes but material to the terminal-share assumption embedded in semicap multiples. The policy leg is the underrated catalyst: House Resolution 8170 would statutorily restrict SMIC, Hua Hong, CXMT, Huawei and YMTC — three of which are the first domestic-scanner customers — and the MATCH Act, reported out of committee 22 April, covers servicing and technical assistance, reaching ASML tools already installed in Chinese fabs; if servicing is cut, the domestic tool ramp becomes a substitution accelerant rather than a curiosity, which is the scenario that would justify a durable de-rate across BESI, CAMT and ONTO's China lines. A domestic EUV effort exists but is still a prototype.
  Sources: [timesofindia.indiatimes.com](https://timesofindia.indiatimes.com/technology/tech-news/mid-2030s-at-the-earliest-analysts-wrote-off-chinas-chipmaking-ambitions-now-the-first-homegrown-machines-ship-this-year/articleshow/132681063.cms)

For coverage it is a demand signal for the AMD rack supply chain (VRT power/cooling, HBM at 000660/285A, MRVL/AVGO adjacent networking is not implicated here) and a reminder that Core Scientific's asset base remains the contested prize after its failed CRWV combination. Confirm with disclosed capacity phasing/GW-online dates and MI455X shipment timing; refute if the 2.5GW is a non-binding option with no committed prepay. *(headline only)*

Confirmation would be Microsoft/Amazon/Alphabet raising FY capex or 2027 commentary in that window; refutation would be a guide that holds capex flat while third-party leasing accelerates, which would imply substitution rather than incremental demand.

- `5` **CSGP Looks 70.7% Undervalued on GF Value™** → [[Theses/CSGP - CoStar Group]]
  CoStar guided Q3 revenue to $935–945M; the rest of the item is GuruFocus 'GF Value' boilerplate claiming CSGP is 70.7% undervalued and carries no incremental fundamental content. No read-through to the AI supply-chain theses; treat as a placeholder until the actual Q2 print detail (Homes.com spend, net new bookings) is available. *(headline only)*
  Sources: [www.gurufocus.com](https://www.gurufocus.com/news/8984147/csgp-looks-707-undervalued-on-gf-value)
- `5` **China's Lithography Breakthrough Sends Chip Stocks Lower While Experts Question Impact | IBTimes** → [[Theses/SOI - Soitec]]
  Shanghai Aishengna Electronic Technology Group has begun producing domestic immersion DUV scanners for SMIC, Hua Hong and CXMT, knocking ASML down as much as 8% and dragging BESI -8.5%, Soitec -5% and Infineon -3%. The scale check argues the market overreacted: The Information puts Aishengna at ~5 units in 2026 and ~20 in 2027 against ASML's ~130 immersion machines planned for 2026 plus a 30% capacity add in 2027, and ODDO BHF's Houri calls the output potentially 'limited to the very low end' with EUV 'out of reach.' For coverage, the direct hit is BESI — sold off on read-through, not on any change to its hybrid-bonding/AI packaging demand (see items 2/3 where advanced packaging is described as accelerating), which suggests the BESI move is a mispricing unless China exposure is material to its backlog. The wfe-china transmission to CAMT, ONTO, AMAT and LRCX is second-order and slower: domestic DUV only matters to their China revenue if Chinese fabs pair it with domestic inspection/deposition, so watch qualification news out of SMIC/CXMT lines rather than the tool announcement itself.
  Sources: [www.ibtimes.com](https://www.ibtimes.com/china-lithography-asml-chip-stocks-impact-3805826)
- `5` **Taiwan Prosecutors Reportedly Detain Nvidia Staffer In China AI Chip Smuggling Probe**
  Taiwan's Keelung District Prosecutors detained an Nvidia employee (surname Chang) on suspicion of multiple offenses including document falsification, after searching his home and Nvidia's Taipei office; this is the third wave of a probe that already detained several Super Micro employees in May and June, and follows the March DOJ indictment of SMCI co-founder Yih-Shyan 'Wally' Liaw over an alleged $2.5B diversion of Nvidia-powered servers to China via a Southeast Asian intermediary. Prosecutors explicitly do not accuse Nvidia or any company of wrongdoing, and Nvidia says smuggling is a 'nonstarter' and diverted chips get 'no service, support or updates.' For the export-controls question this is enforcement escalating from US paperwork into allied-jurisdiction criminal detentions of vendor personnel — the mechanism that matters is channel friction: if gray-market resale routes through Taiwan/SE Asia are being closed, it trims an unmeasured slice of NVDA demand while structurally strengthening the domestic-substitution case for Huawei Ascend (huawei-ascend) and Chinese WFE build-out (wfe-china). Headline/legal risk for NVDA is reputational rather than financial on current facts; watch for any BIS rulemaking on transshipment or an indictment naming a corporate entity, which would move this from noise to a real compliance-cost item.
  Sources: [forbes.com](https://www.forbes.com/sites/siladityaray/2026/07/28/taiwan-prosecutors-reportedly-detain-nvidia-staffer-in-china-ai-chip-smuggling-probe/)
- `4` **KLA-Tencor Corp (KLAC) Earnings: 4Q Revenue Surpasses Estimates with Strong Product and Service Growth | Smartkarma** → [[Theses/KLA - KLA Corporation]]
  Sell-side commentary on the same KLA print (Keating, Baptista) flags strong 2027 visibility, an accelerating advanced-packaging market and robust WFE spend, following 17% revenue growth to a record in 2025. If advanced packaging is genuinely accelerating in KLA's order book, that corroborates the CoWoS/panel-level (CoPoS) capacity build thesis and supports BESI, ONTO, CAMT and TER on inspection/bonding/test intensity per AI die. This is analyst framing rather than disclosed backlog numbers, so treat 'accelerating' as inference until KLA quantifies advanced-packaging revenue dollars; the confirming datapoint would be TSMC's own capex/packaging commentary and BESI hybrid-bonding order commentary. *(headline only)*
  Sources: [www.smartkarma.com](https://www.smartkarma.com/home/newswire/earnings-alerts/kla-tencor-corp-klac-earnings-4q-revenue-surpasses-estimates-with-strong-product-and-service-growth/)
